{"parentUuid":"31d61d7e-6fa6-451e-a13b-8d7396b7346f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"087400fb-8173-483a-91fe-ccaa67ffaa7b","agentId":"ae24d09dc33db3b10","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011XAmdbZ4DatkzQcaqYqFdK","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tpaths:\n3\t  - '**/*.{ts,tsx,mts,cts,vue}'\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# Naming and API-shape rules\n7\t\n8\tNames are public API. A consumer should be able to predict them without documentation.\n9\t\n10\t## Entity-scoped names: one word\n11\t\n12\tProperties/getters, methods, option keys, and event names belong to an entity whose type already supplies context. Use one descriptive word:\n13\t\n14\t```ts\n15\tinterface AgentInterface {\n16\t\treadonly id: string\n17\t\treadonly context: AgentContextInterface\n18\t\treadonly status: AgentStatus\n19\t\tgenerate(): Promise<string>\n20\t\tstream(options?: AgentStreamOptions): AsyncGenerator<string, string>\n21\t\tabort(): void\n22\t}\n23\t```\n24\t\n25\tHard targets:\n26\t\n27\t- Public properties: one word.\n28\t- Public methods: one word; use two only when no single verb is accurate.\n29\t- Ungrouped option keys: one word.\n30\t- Grouped option key: the configured entity noun; every leaf remains one word.\n31\t- Events: one present-tense verb or noun.\n32\t- Private methods: two or three words are acceptable.\n33\t\n34\tThe rule does not apply to standalone helpers, type names with role suffixes, or qualified constants.\n35\t\n36\t## Split instead of compounding\n37\t\n38\tA compound entity member usually means the API contains multiple entities or behaviors. Do not create `addTool`, `removeDocument`, `databasePath`, or `serverTimeout`.\n39\t\n40\t### Group options by entity\n41\t\n42\t```ts\n43\tinterface ServerOptions {\n44\t\treadonly server?: { readonly port?: number; readonly timeout?: number }\n45\t\treadonly database?: { readonly path?: string; readonly timeout?: number }\n46\t}\n47\t```\n48\t\n49\tNever flatten these into prefixed keys.\n50\t\n51\t### Extract sub-entities\n52\t\n53\tMove prefixed method families to a manager and expose it as a noun:\n54\t\n55\t```ts\n56\tinterface AgentContextInterface {\n57\t\treadonly instructions: InstructionManagerInterface\n58\t\treadonly documents: DocumentManagerInterface\n59\t\treadonly tools: ToolManagerInterface\n60\t}\n61\t```\n62\t\n63\tCall `context.instructions.add(input)`, not `context.addInstruction(input)`.\n64\t\n65\t### Split behavioral variants\n66\t\n67\tDo not hide multiple algorithms behind a discriminator parameter:\n68\t\n69\t```ts\n70\tparseJSON(input)\n71\tparseYAML(input)\n72\tparseTOML(input)\n73\t```\n74\t\n75\tDo not write `parse(input, format)`. A literal that selects a different action is a magic mode and requires separate functions/methods.\n76\t\n77\tDistinguish behavior from data:\n78\t\n79\t- Different value selects a different action/algorithm/shape of work → split.\n80\t- Different value selects a datum for the same operation → keep it as data.\n81\t- Discriminants, reasoning/error codes, uniformly applied value enums, and field/key selectors are data.\n82\t\n83\t## Standalone helpers\n84\t\n85\tModule helpers have no owning entity at the call site, so default to `{verb}{Noun}`:\n86\t\n87\t`generateId`, `computeHash`, `extractColumns`, `derivePhaseStatus`, `inferLanguage`, `normalizePath`, `sanitizeFilename`, `matchesGlobPattern`, `belongsTo`, `hasMany`.\n88\t\n89\t- A one-word helper is valid only when its meaning and arguments are unmistakable: `delay`, `clamp`, `tokenize`, `similarity`.\n90\t- Reject vague helpers such as `process` or `handle`.\n91\t- A helper prefix has one project-wide meaning: `extract*` extracts structure, `infer*` derives, `compute*` calculates deterministically, and `matches*` is a predicate.\n92\t- When a helper family grows around one shape, promote it to a class with entity-scoped one-word methods.\n93\t\n94\t## General vocabulary\n95\t\n96\tThe root design laws in `AGENTS.md` — one term per concept, boolean behavior switches, `undefined` absence, derived state, real domain states, and named discriminants — bind here. This section adds only their naming specifics:\n97\t\n98\t- Describe what a thing is, not its implementation.\n99\t- Prefer short common English; avoid jargon, abbreviations, and non-universal acronyms.\n100\t- Properties are nouns; methods are verbs.\n101\t- Booleans read as assertions: `aborted`, `exhausted`, `expired`.\n102\t- Accessors use bare nouns, never `get*`/`set*`.\n103\t- Do not alternate `count`/`length`/`size`/`total` or `abort`/`cancel`.\n104\t- Name the axis a discriminant varies: `relationship`, `command`, `category`, `operation`, `via`.\n105\t- A binary switch is a boolean such as `bail`, never `'continue' | 'halt'`; genuine discriminants, multi-state lifecycles, conventional value pairs (`ascending`/`descending`, `and`/`or`), and external-spec literals remain unions.\n106\t\n107\t## Acronyms\n108\t\n109\tKeep canonical case:\n110\t\n111\t- Initialisms: `JSON`, `HTTP`, `NDJSON`, `SSE`, `URL`, `URI`, `SQL`, `API`, `DB`, `TTL`, `UUID`, `RFC`, `JWT`.\n112\t- Brands/proper names: `SQLite`, `IndexedDB`, `GitHub`, `OAuth`.\n113\t- PascalCase identifiers preserve the acronym: `JSONSchema`, `HTTPServer`, `NDJSONParser`, `SSEStream`, `URLPattern`, `SQLiteDriver`, `IndexedDBDriver`.\n114\t- camelCase lowers an acronym only when it leads: `jsonValue`, `urlPath`, `sqlText`; otherwise preserve it: `parseJSON`, `toJSON`, `fromNDJSON`, `signJWT`, `compileSQL`.\n115\t- Ecosystem-conventional `id` and leading `url`/`uri` remain lowercase.\n116\t- Domain folders remain lowercase: `http/`, `sqlite/`, `indexeddb/`, `websocket/`.\n117\t- Never title-fold canonical acronyms into `Json`, `Ndjson`, `Http`, `Sse`, or `Sqlite`.\n118\t\n119\t## Type-level identifiers\n120\t\n121\t| Kind                      | Required form                           |\n122\t| ------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |\n123\t| Behavioral interface      | `{Entity}Interface`                     |\n124\t| Options/config            | `{Entity}Options`                       |\n125\t| Creation input            | `{Entity}Input`                         |\n126\t| Outcome/output            | `{Entity}Result`                        |\n127\t| Execution context         | `{Entity}Context`                       |\n128\t| Event map                 | `{Entity}EventMap`                      |\n129\t| Union/enum-like           | `{Entity}{Noun}`                        |\n130\t| Plain non-behavioral data | `{Entity}`                              |\n131\t| Function type             | `{Entity}Handler` or `{Entity}Function` |\n132\t| Manager interface         | `{Entity}ManagerInterface`              |\n133\t\n134\t- Never prefix interfaces with `I`.\n135\t- Never pluralize type names.\n136\t- `Handler` is for function types, never classes.\n137\t\n138\t## Value-level identifiers\n139\t\n140\t| Kind           | Required form                         |\n141\t| -------------- | ------------------------------------- |\n142\t| Class          | PascalCase `{Entity}`                 |\n143\t| Manager class  | PascalCase `{Entity}Manager`          |\n144\t| Factory        | camelCase `create{Entity}`            |\n145\t| Guard          | camelCase `is{Condition}`             |\n146\t| Helper         | camelCase `{verb}{Noun}`              |\n147\t| Constant       | UPPER_SNAKE_CASE `{QUALIFIER}_{NOUN}` |\n148\t| Property/field | camelCase bare noun                   |\n149\t| Method         | camelCase bare verb                   |\n150\t| Boolean        | camelCase adjective/past participle   |\n151\t\n152\t## Fixed derivation/construction forms\n153\t\n154\t- `is*`: total `Guard<T>`; never throws; returns false off-shape.\n155\t- `parse*`: coercion producing `T | undefined`; cross-type conversion never belongs in a guard.\n156\t- `create*`: factory constructing an entity/value.\n157\t- `*Of`: builder combining constituent parts into a container/guard/value, such as `arrayOf(guard)` or `boundsOf(min, max)`.\n158\t- `{noun}To{Noun}`: projection from a whole to a derived view, such as `definitionToSnapshot`.\n159\t- `*Shape`: `ContractShape` value/JSON-Schema blueprint, not a function or type.\n160\t- Leading `_`: intentionally unused binding only; never privacy.\n161\t\n162\tFor `_` bindings:\n163\t\n164\t- Use only for genuine callback/signature conformance, rest omission, swallowed catches, or intentionally unused loop variables.\n165\t- Verify each use is intentional; remove `_` and wire the value if it should be consumed.\n166\t- Remove the parameter when signature compatibility does not require it.\n167\t- Prefer a short justification for each rare `_` in `src/`.\n168\t\n169\t## Tallies\n170\t\n171\t- A lone unambiguous tally is `count`.\n172\t- When several distinct tallies coexist, name each fact: a pool may expose `size`, `idle`, and `active`; a queue may expose `count` and `active`.\n173\t- Do not expose several ambiguous `count` properties.\n174\t\n175\t## Files and folders\n176\t\n177\t| Kind            | Pattern                                                                          |\n178\t| --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n179\t| Domain folder   | lowercase plural entity: `agents/`, `tools/`                                     |\n180\t| Implementation  | PascalCase entity: `Agent.ts`                                                    |\n181\t| Function module | camelCase function: `renderGrid.ts`                                              |\n182\t| Test            | source filename without extension + `.test`: `Agent.test.ts`, `useTheme.test.ts` |\n183\t| Guide           | lowercase domain: `agents.md`                                                    |\n184\t\n185\t## Fixed lifecycle vocabulary\n186\t\n187\t| Verb      | Exact meaning                             |\n188\t| --------- | ----------------------------------------- |\n189\t| `start`   | Begin or restart                          |\n190\t| `stop`    | End permanently                           |\n191\t| `pause`   | Suspend resumably                         |\n192\t| `resume`  | Continue after pause                      |\n193\t| `skip`    | Mark intentionally unexecuted             |\n194\t| `abort`   | Cancel with signal propagation            |\n195\t| `clear`   | Reset state without destroying the entity |\n196\t| `destroy` | Tear down and release resources           |\n197\t| `execute` | Run primary work to completion            |\n198\t\n199\tNever introduce synonyms such as `cancel`, `reset`, or `run` for these meanings.\n200\t\n201\t## Rejected naming\n202\t\n203\t- Generic words: `data`, `info`, `item`, `thing`, `obj`.\n204\t- Type-encoded names: `nameString`, `countNumber`.\n205\t- Abbreviations: `cfg`, `doc`, `msg`; write `config`, `document`, `message`.\n206\t- `@internal` methods; use `#` privacy.\n207\t- Compound entity members where grouping/extraction/splitting provides the context.\n208\t- Behavior-selecting magic strings.\n209\t"}]},"uuid":"abf7c5f3-d6d8-44df-aae9-38549bca4f2f","timestamp":"2026-08-26T08:05:18.050Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"31d61d7e-6fa6-451e-a13b-8d7396b7346f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote_mobile","cwd":"/home/user/markdown","sessionId":"e44afe43-d783-57c4-9b94-e1b722b0b4a2","version":"2.1.246","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"zany-launching-leaf"}
{"parentUuid":"e9424170-e244-410b-a38e-881fc15a2d34","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"087400fb-8173-483a-91fe-ccaa67ffaa7b","agentId":"ae24d09dc33db3b10","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VQ4uhYSrvXn1F5EC8B3QXg","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tpaths:\n3\t  - 'tests/**/*'\n4\t  - 'vite.config.ts'\n5\t  - 'configs/**/*.ts'\n6\t  - 'package.json'\n7\t---\n8\t\n9\t# Testing rules\n10\t\n11\t## Test contract\n12\t\n13\t- Mirror module/application structure:\n14\t  `tests/{src,app}/[environment]/[domain]/[module].test.ts`.\n15\t- The mirrored population is `src` and `app` alone. `configs/` is a source directory and is\n16\t  deliberately not a mirrored root: its leaves produce the workspace's configuration rather than ship\n17\t  in it, and they are proved from `tests/config.test.ts` beside the configuration they produce. Do\n18\t  not add `tests/configs/`.\n19\t- Resolve a mirrored module through `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, `.cts`, `.vue`, `.scss`, or `.css`.\n20\t- Resolve a Sass or CSS partial through the module's leading underscore.\n21\t- Resolve each root `tests/setup*.test.ts` proof against its sibling `tests/setup*.ts` module. A\n22\t  root `tests/setup.test.ts` file can prove several setup modules when their helpers serve\n23\t  several projects.\n24\t- Prefer test filenames matching entrypoints: `index.test.ts` for `index.ts`, `main.test.ts` for `main.ts`.\n25\t- Tests are deterministic: identical inputs produce identical results.\n26\t- Keep default suites fast: timers normally use 10–50 ms and tests make no network calls.\n27\t- Use real implementations and small scenarios. Never use mocks, behavioral fakes, module replacement, or framework spies for project-owned or integrated behavior.\n28\t- Use recorders for calls/events, temporary resources for stateful boundaries, protocol-faithful fixture servers for deterministic network peers, and the real external service when its behavior is the claim.\n29\t- Prefer inert customizable data and input stubs.\n30\t- Allow a scripted boundary stub only when it implements the real interface or protocol minimally, to drive the system under test. It never reimplements project-owned behavior and never stands in for the integration being claimed.\n31\t- Bind a test fixture server to `127.0.0.1` on an ephemeral port (`listen(0)`), never to `::1` and never to a fixed port: a host without IPv6 fails `EAFNOSUPPORT`, and a fixed port flakes on occupancy.\n32\t- Cover happy paths, error paths, empty input, boundary values, `NaN`, positive/negative zero, cycles, and Map/Set order where relevant.\n33\t- Test observable behavior, not implementation details.\n34\t- Assert the membership a discovered or globbed set should have, not a total that a partly empty population satisfies. A glob spanning two locations passes a size check while one of them matches nothing.\n35\t- Never assert an implementation against itself. Compare the answer to a declaration, a fixture, or a second mechanism that could disagree with it. Re-deriving the answer the same way the source derives it produces a test that passes for every value the source ever returns, and it reads exactly like a real one.\n36\t- Probe a host-varying property at runtime, on the host the test is running on, and assert against what the probe returned. Filesystem case folding, path separators, permission bits, and rename semantics differ per host, so a fixture built on one host describes that host and silently measures something else on the next.\n37\t- Assert a runtime-chosen result as the property it must have, not as the number one run produced. Compression, timing, and buffer sizing are the runtime's choice, so pin the relationship the test depends on — that the encoded form is larger, that the second call is faster — and let the assertion fail when the input drifts out of the range where that relationship holds.\n38\t- Measure an elapsed interval with `performance.now()`, never `Date.now()`. `Date.now()` returns whole milliseconds, so an interval built from two of its readings truncates at both ends and can under-report by a millisecond — enough to fail a boundary assertion against a timer that behaved correctly. `performance.now()` is monotonic and sub-millisecond, and it does not move when the wall clock does.\n39\t- Give a conditional skip the mechanism that makes it inapplicable, cited, not the platform name alone. A test skipped on a platform is a test nobody re-examines; a test skipped because a named API rejects a named case is one anybody can re-check.\n40\t- A regression test records the exact command and its failing count before the fix, and the same command's passing count after.\n41\t- The revert that proves a repair reddens exactly the test that names the defect. Keep the import and collection graph valid while reverting, and confirm the named test was collected. A revert that reddens anything beyond that test broke the harness, and its count is not evidence.\n42\t- Use `it.todo()` only for explicitly out-of-scope roadmap work, never to complete the current request. Every `.skip` or conditional skip has a narrow verifiable applicability reason.\n43\t- Do not create test files solely for `constants.ts`, barrels, error definitions, or `types.ts`.\n44\t- Run the narrowest relevant Vitest project during development; do not run the entire suite casually.\n45\t\n46\t## Cross-cutting proofs\n47\t\n48\tA proof that covers the workspace instead of one module has a fixed location, so no package invents\n49\tits own:\n50\t\n51\t| Path                         | Proves                                                                                                                         |\n52\t| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n53\t| `tests/policy.test.ts`       | Every source file obeys the syntactic coding and placement law                                                                 |\n54\t| `tests/config.test.ts`       | Root configuration resolves its aliases, projects, and outputs, and the `configs/` leaves behind them                          |\n55\t| `tests/guides.test.ts`       | Every documented API exists, every public API is documented, and every executable fence returns what the guide says it returns |\n56\t| `tests/conformance.test.ts`  | Where this package drifts from the official tooling it tracks                                                                  |\n57\t| `tests/distribution.test.ts` | The packed package installs and resolves through its public exports                                                            |\n58\t| `tests/integration.test.ts`  | The package's features work together end to end across environments                                                            |\n59\t| `tests/setup*.test.ts`       | Reusable behavior exported from sibling `tests/setup*.ts` modules works as the workspace's suites require                      |\n60\t| `tests/service/**/*.test.ts` | The live external services this package drives, driven for real                                                                |\n61\t\n62\t- Put each root `tests/setup*.test.ts` proof in the `setup` project. Keep its assertions on\n63\t  exported test-infrastructure behavior: do not duplicate production behavior there, and do not\n64\t  move setup-helper assertions into another cross-cutting proof.\n65\t- `.claude/rules/workspace.md` names the Vitest project each location belongs to.\n66\t- The `guides` project runs in Node with the browser disabled. Its subject is what the guide\n67\t  claims: that every documented name resolves, and that every fence asserting a value returns\n68\t  that value. A proof that renders a component and compares it against a definition is a\n69\t  composition and belongs in an `integration.test.ts` scoped to its directory.\n70\t- Transcribe each flagship fence and assert the values its comments claim. Name resolution is\n71\t  not a behavioural proof, so a fence documenting a value the code contradicts passes every\n72\t  parity assertion. Change a fence, change the transcription beside it.\n73\t- `integration.test.ts` is a reserved filename at any level. It names a scope rather than a module,\n74\t  so the mirror rule does not reach it; its scope is the directory it sits in.\n75\t- An integration test is an end-to-end test: it composes the package's own features and drives them\n76\t  together, through the public API, with no part of the system under test replaced.\n77\t- Scope it by where the composition happens. `tests/integration.test.ts` drives features **across**\n78\t  environments — core through server, core through browser, one environment's output consumed by\n79\t  another. A nested `tests/src/<environment>/integration.test.ts` drives features **within** that one\n80\t  environment.\n81\t- Do not put a packaging, install, or distribution check in an integration test. What the tarball\n82\t  contains is a different question from whether the features compose.\n83\t- A test the mirror rule flags is a misplaced test until its placement is checked. Move it to the\n84\t  location its scope names. Widen the rule only when that check shows the test already sits at the\n85\t  location its scope names and the rule's population omits a module this ruleset mandates, and then\n86\t  widen the population rather than admitting the individual case.\n87\t- A nested `tests/{src,app}/<environment>/**/integration.test.ts` runs in that environment's project,\n88\t  whose existing glob collects it exactly once. Give it a separate exact-path project entry only when\n89\t  the proof needs different setup or a different runtime, and exclude that exact path from the\n90\t  environment project when you do.\n91\t\n92\t## Probes\n93\t\n94\tA probe is a throwaway instrument that settles one question. It is not a test and never ships.\n95\t\n96\tRoute the question before writing a probe. When you can state the edit you believe is correct and the\n97\tedit that must break with the stage it breaks at, the question is a claim for the `prove` tool the\n98\t`probe` MCP server registers, and `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Instruments owns that rule. When the\n99\tquestion carries no stated belief to falsify yet, when the subject lies outside the TypeScript a\n100\tworkspace project judges, or when the proof needs what that tool's stages do not model — a process\n101\ttree, a listening socket, an installed package, a built entry driven as a child, or a live external\n102\tservice — write a probe.\n103\t\n104\tThe kinds split by which tool has to see the probe:\n105\t\n106\t- A **type probe** is read by `tsc`, whose scoped project includes only its own environment, so it\n107\t  lives in the source tree beside what it measures. Delete it before the unit returns; a leaked one\n108\t  fails the placement sweep, because a probe filename is not a centralized kind file.\n109\t- A **runtime probe** is collected by a Vitest project, so it lives in `tmp/probe/` and runs through\n110\t  the `probe` project. `tmp/` is ignored by git, so no probe enters a commit by accident, and every\n111\t  test script names its project, so no gate runs the `probe` project.\n112\t- A **bench** is read by Vitest's benchmark mode, so it lives inside a test file as a block behind\n113\t  the `if (import.meta.env.MODE === 'benchmark')` guard. Only the `test:bench` script collects the\n114\t  block, test mode fails an unguarded `bench()` call loudly, and no gate runs a bench. Call `bench`\n115\t  directly inside the guard. A `describe` inside it trips `vitest/no-conditional-tests`, and a suite\n116\t  the guard leaves unregistered fails test mode with `No test found in suite`.\n117\t\n118\tRun a probe before relying on an unverified belief about behaviour: what a function returns, what a\n119\tconfiguration resolves to, whether a path is reached at all. Prefer a probe to an argument whenever\n120\tthe probe is cheap.\n121\t\n122\tWhen the question is whether the difference between methods is a magnitude or negligible, write a\n123\tguarded bench block beside the probe test and run the `test:bench` script. Declare the threshold\n124\tbefore the run, read the ratio between the methods against each side's reported uncertainty, and\n125\trecord nothing below a magnitude. A settled magnitude that underwrites an implementation choice\n126\tpromotes with its test into the mirrored suite and keeps its guarded block there while that choice\n127\tstands. A deterministic relationship promotes as an ordinary assertion, so delete the block. Never\n128\tcommit baseline output.\n129\t\n130\tThese rules bind every probe:\n131\t\n132\t- **Prove the instrument can fail before trusting that it passed.** Pair it with a control drawn\n133\t  from outside the population it covers.\n134\t- **Promote or delete.** A probe that settles a claim becomes a test in the mirrored location that\n135\t  proves the real source. Delete every other probe. A probe left in the suite reports on the harness\n136\t  while reading as a test.\n137\t- **Never commit a probe.** The ignore rule stops an accident, not a deliberate forced add, and a\n138\t  type probe sits in tracked source with no ignore rule at all.\n139\t\n140\t## Live-service tests\n141\t\n142\tLive external services/models are the deliberate exception to fast hermetic defaults:\n143\t\n144\t- Put them in the `service` project, under `tests/service/`, with `tests/setupService.ts` for setup\n145\t  and a longer timeout. That module's presence is what registers the project, so a live proof with\n146\t  no readiness setup is a project nothing configures.\n147\t- `.claude/rules/workspace.md` fixes which gate runs the `service` project, and that gate is not the\n148\t  same one in a publishing and a `private: true` workspace.\n149\t- Warm and verify service readiness in `tests/setupService.ts`.\n150\t- Hard-require readiness: throw loudly; never silently skip.\n151\t- Verify service-dependent logic through that service's project, not unrelated module tests or scattered conditional skips.\n152\t- Tune each request to the smallest input/context/output that proves one behavior without becoming brittle or expensive.\n153\t- Prefer semantic bounded assertions over exact generated prose. Increase context/workload only when the scenario requires it.\n154\t\n155\t## Expensive proofs\n156\t\n157\tA test that spawns a process, packs, installs, or drives a real build is a proof, not a unit test.\n158\t\n159\t- Give it its own Vitest project with its own setup and timeout.\n160\t- Keep it out of the default run where the workspace has a gate that can hold it.\n161\t  `.claude/rules/workspace.md` fixes which gate each isolated project runs from, and that placement\n162\t  differs between a publishing and a `private: true` workspace.\n163\t- The fixed expensive-proof projects are `distribution` and `service`.\n164\t- A `distribution` proof reads `import.meta.env.MODE` and fails, rather than skips, on an unreachable\n165\t  registry under `--mode release`. The publish gate invokes it that way, so a proof that skips there\n166\t  passes the gate without ever proving the artifact installs. An ordinary local run may still skip.\n167\t- Slow and hermetic is reason enough to isolate a proof; it need not touch an external service.\n168\t- Where such a proof stays in a shared project, size its budget from a full contended run rather than from an isolated one. A budget that clears the isolated cost by a thin margin turns contention into a red gate reporting a timeout, which carries no diagnostic about the code and costs a full investigation to dismiss.\n169\t\n170\t## Shared test infrastructure\n171\t\n172\tTest helpers are shared infrastructure, not local test-file clutter.\n173\t\n174\t`@orkestrel/test` owns the helpers every workspace repeats: the call recorder, the real delay, the JSON and async collectors, and the owned scratch directory. Import them from `@orkestrel/test`, and its Node-only helpers from `@orkestrel/test/server`. Write a helper of your own only where the package exports none for the job. The following shapes are the contract a workspace codes against, not source to copy.\n175\t\n176\t- For the vendored test set (`tests/setupPolicy.ts`, `tests/policy.test.ts`, and\n177\t  `tests/config.test.ts`), keep shared helpers within that set instead of importing them from\n178\t  `@orkestrel/test`; follow the vendored-file import law in `.claude/rules/workspace.md`.\n179\t\n180\t- Extract a fixture, recorder, event factory, async wait, renderer, scenario/data builder, protocol fixture, or DOM builder as soon as it could serve another test.\n181\t- Any duplicate or near-duplicate helper is a defect; consolidate it into one general form.\n182\t- Export every reusable helper, fixture type, factory, constant, and guard from setup files.\n183\t- A setup file owns everything an assertion needs and nothing an assertion is: `describe`, `it`, and `expect` never appear in a `setup*.ts`.\n184\t- Data tables and case matrices belong in a setup file at any size; test registration does not.\n185\t- Test files import shared infrastructure rather than declaring local fixture factories.\n186\t- Never reimplement a framework helper in tests or fixtures; import the real parser, signer, flattener, or other helper.\n187\t- Prefer small customizable factories/stubs that seed inert data for a real scenario over repeated inline setup.\n188\t- Helper names follow module-helper naming: `createFixtureServer`, `buildElement`, `appendItems`, `renderRows`, `waitForReady`, `extractDetail`.\n189\t\n190\tPlace helpers by environment:\n191\t\n192\t- `tests/setup.ts`: host-independent; no `node:*`, DOM, `window`, or Vue.\n193\t- `tests/setupServer.ts`: Node-only helpers and `node:fs` loaders anchored to `WORKSPACE_ROOT`.\n194\t- `tests/setupBrowser.ts`: DOM/Vue/browser helpers and setup CSS.\n195\t- `tests/setupStyles.ts`: CSS/style helpers and compiled cascade.\n196\t\n197\t### Recorder\n198\t\n199\tImport `createRecorder` from `@orkestrel/test` instead of a framework spy when only calls and arguments matter. It returns:\n200\t\n201\t```ts\n202\tinterface RecorderInterface<TArgs extends readonly unknown[]> {\n203\t\treadonly calls: readonly TArgs[]\n204\t\treadonly count: number\n205\t\treadonly handler: (...args: TArgs) => void\n206\t\tclear(): void\n207\t}\n208\t```\n209\t\n210\t### Delay\n211\t\n212\tImport `waitForDelay` from `@orkestrel/test`; never repeat an inline timeout promise. It waits for one host timer and defaults to `0`:\n213\t\n214\t```ts\n215\tfunction waitForDelay(ms?: number): Promise<void>\n216\t```\n217\t\n218\tUse it to yield, never to wait for something another process produces. A fixed delay chosen to\n219\toutlast a child's startup is a race whose loss looks like a product defect: the test measures\n220\tinterpreter bootstrap rather than the behaviour it names, and it fails on a loaded host and passes on\n221\tan idle one. Wait until a named condition holds instead, polling with `waitForDelay` inside a budget\n222\tmeasured by `performance.now()`, and fail with the condition's own description when the budget\n223\texpires.\n224\t\n225\t### Scratch\n226\t\n227\tImport `createScratch` from `@orkestrel/test/server` when a proof needs real files. It allocates a temporary directory it owns, contains every path against escape, and removes the directory on `destroy`:\n228\t\n229\t```ts\n230\tinterface ScratchInterface {\n231\t\treadonly path: string\n232\t\twrite(target: string, text: string): string\n233\t\tread(target: string): string | undefined\n234\t\thas(target: string): boolean\n235\t\tnames(target?: string): readonly string[]\n236\t\tensure(target: string): string\n237\t\tlink(target: string, source: string): string\n238\t\tremove(target: string): void\n239\t\tdestroy(): void\n240\t}\n241\t```\n242\t\n243\t### Style primitives\n244\t\n245\tBrowser/style setup exposes shared assertions/builders:\n246\t\n247\t`mount`, `render`, `build`, `style`, `token`, `rootToken`, `pixels`, `rgba`, `colorEqual`, `findRule`.\n248\t\n249\t`findRule` proves a declaration exists in the cascade; `style()` reads the resolved result.\n250\t\n251\t## Browser tests\n252\t\n253\tUse the real browser as the system under test:\n254\t\n255\t- Do not replace DOM events, storage, observers, viewports, layout methods, pointer, or drag APIs unless the browser genuinely lacks one.\n256\t- Prefer real nodes, events, styles/layout, and observers.\n257\t- Centralize event factories: `createPointerEvent`, `createDragEvent`, `typeInput`, `fireTransitionEnd`.\n258\t- Centralize DOM builders: `createButtonElement`, `createDropdownElements`, `createModalElement`.\n259\t- Assert DOM state, emitted events, callback records, focus, classes, attributes, and public API state.\n260\t- Do not assert private state, internal timers, or framework scheduler internals.\n261\t- Prefer real short timers and observable wakeups. Never replace the host clock.\n262\t\n263\t## Runner configuration\n264\t\n265\tKeep Vitest/provider configuration minimal:\n266\t\n267\t- Prefer defaults until a measured problem proves them insufficient.\n268\t- Centralize provider setup in one helper/shared block.\n269\t- Add browser/provider/teardown/timeout/parallelism/cache/launch settings only for a current verified need.\n270\t- Avoid long browser flag lists and persistent contexts unless a test requires them.\n271\t- For slow teardown, inspect test cleanup, open handles, file parallelism, and context churn before adding launch flags.\n272\t- Remove exploratory settings after fixing the cause.\n273\t- Config comments explain the current reason, not the history of failed experiments.\n274\t\n275\t## Untestable usually means missing seam\n276\t\n277\tBefore accepting that a behavior cannot be tested, look for the seam that would make it testable.\n278\t\n279\t- Treat a collaborator reached through a hard-coded global — a stream, a clock source, a spawn, a fetch — as a missing injection point, not an untestable truth.\n280\t- An injected collaborator with a real minimal implementation is a sanctioned boundary stub, not a mock of project-owned behavior.\n281\t- Add the seam rather than shipping the gap whenever the design would welcome it anyway, and whenever a sibling collaborator is already injected.\n282\t- Record a genuinely irreducible gap where a reader meets it: what is unproven, why it cannot be driven, and what would change that. A silent untested guard reads exactly like a tested one.\n283\t\n284\tCoverage rules:\n285\t\n286\t- Read a coverage report as a discovery input, never as evidence of proof. It answers one cheap mechanical question — which code no test executed — and that reliably finds forgotten branches and rules nothing calls.\n287\t- Never cite coverage as the adequacy audit's result. It cannot tell you whether an executed line is asserted, so a fully covered file can still be entirely unproven.\n288\t- Never let a percentage become the target.\n289\t\n290\t## Discovery and adequacy audit\n291\t\n292\tBefore acceptance:\n293\t\n294\t- prove every intended test file is discovered by the correct project;\n295\t- prove every declared project is reachable from a gate. A project registered in the root\n296\t  configuration with no script, or with a script no chain runs, is a proof that never executes — and\n297\t  because it never executes it never fails, so the suite reports green while carrying it. Read the\n298\t  chain, not the exit code: run each project directly once and compare that list against what `test`\n299\t  actually invokes. The gate that would report this gap is the gate that is missing.\n300\t- prove a declared project's include resolves to a real file. An empty project is not a passing\n301\t  project: Vitest exits non-zero on \"no test files found\", so a project aimed at a path that was\n302\t  never created stays invisible until something finally runs it.\n303\t- inspect actual test counts and environments;\n304\t- audit `.todo`, `.skip`, conditional skips, retries, and inflated timeouts;\n305\t- confirm each assertion would fail for the defect it claims to catch, and that it fails rather than passes when its population is empty;\n306\t- confirm helpers do not reimplement production behavior;\n307\t- confirm cleanup runs after setup or assertion failure;\n308\t- confirm current-scope requirements have real tests rather than placeholders.\n309\t"}]},"uuid":"1fdc3ea6-ec2b-4237-82da-d3837ac839a2","timestamp":"2026-08-26T08:05:18.053Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e9424170-e244-410b-a38e-881fc15a2d34","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote_mobile","cwd":"/home/user/markdown","sessionId":"e44afe43-d783-57c4-9b94-e1b722b0b4a2","version":"2.1.246","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"zany-launching-leaf"}

The\n6\tOrchestrator reconciles; the rulings below are the plan.\n7\t\n8\t## Where the lanes agree, adopted as ruled\n9\t\n10\t- The entry point: `parseProvenance(markdown): MarkdownParseResult` with\n11\t  `MarkdownParseResult = readonly [document: MarkdownDocument, spans:\n12\t  ReadonlyMap<MarkdownNode, MarkdownSpan>]`, and `parseDocument` as its document-only\n13\t  projection — the lanes returned this contract independently, token for token.\n14\t- `MarkdownSpan { readonly start: number; readonly end: number }`, half-open UTF-16\n15\t  offsets into the ORIGINAL constructor string.\n16\t- The carrier: a handle-local identity map in a `#` field on `Markdown`, read through\n17\t  `span(node): MarkdownSpan | undefined` on `MarkdownInterface`, returning a fresh value;\n18\t  nodes gain no field; an adopted document starts with none; provenance is never\n19\t  process-global or factory-owned.\n20\t- Derivation: `MarkdownDerivation<T> = readonly [value: T, derivations:\n21\t  ReadonlyMap<MarkdownNode, MarkdownNode | undefined>]`; `rewriteDocument` returns it;\n22\t  `Markdown.map` resolves the chain against the source handle's map; an output identity\n23\t  from separate sources maps to `undefined`; consumers update in the same change, no\n24\t  compatibility engine.\n25\t- The no-single-source law, per surface: a `coalesceText` join drops provenance; a\n26\t  one-source rebuild (a trimmed text node, a single-input normalization) may keep its\n27\t  source's span; `mergeProjections`-synthesized paragraphs have none; slot-mismatch and\n28\t  depth-cap identities keep theirs by identity; adopted documents expose none; the\n29\t  html-projection helpers stay inert (their inputs never had spans).\n30\t- The cross-package boundary: excluded, with the analyst's register wording adopted —\n31\t  when markdown's INSTALLED `@orkestrel/html` release exposes handle-relative spans,\n32\t  design an explicit handle-based projection; never infer html spans from bare\n33\t  `HTMLNode` input. The installed release is `0.0.6` and exposes none.\n34\t- The guide promise: spans address the original constructor string; parsed nodes cover\n35\t  consumed syntax; one-source rewrites keep the region through `map`; joins, projections,\n36\t  adopted documents, and synthetic nodes return `undefined`.\n37\t\n38\t## The ruled forks\n39\t\n40\t1. **The coordinate mechanism: the planner's original-walk segments, on probed facts.**\n41\t   The analyst's normalize-with-boundary-map shape presumed a `U+0000` rewrite the parser\n42\t   does not perform: the Orchestrator's probe of 2026-08-26 (`h2-facts-probe.mjs`, run in\n43\t   the markdown tree) reads `parseDocument('a\u0000b')` returning the text value with the\n44\t   null retained, `splitLines('a\\n')` returning `['a']` (the trailing terminator removed,\n45\t   as the analyst read), and CRLF plus lone CR normalizing at line granularity. With no\n46\t   per-character normalization anywhere, walking the original buys original coordinates\n47\t   outright: `splitLines` returns `MarkdownSource` values — `{ readonly text: string;\n48\t   readonly segments: readonly MarkdownSegment[] }` with\n49\t   `MarkdownSegment { readonly offset: number; readonly start: number; readonly end:\n50\t   number }` — and the `sliceSource`, `joinSources`, and `projectSpan` leaves carry every\n51\t   strip, trim, and join, which are piecewise affine at line granularity. The analyst's\n52\t   per-construct threading table (headings, blockquotes, lists, tables, paragraphs,\n53\t   fences, the document span `[0, markdown.length]` including a removed trailing\n54\t   terminator) binds as the acceptance detail for the units; its warning stands as law:\n55\t   never reconstruct offsets from node values.\n56\t   - Register entry, markdown parse conformance: the CommonMark specification replaces\n57\t     `U+0000` with `U+FFFD` as an insecure character; this parser retains it (the probe\n58\t     above). Whether the package owes the replacement is its own ruled change, outside\n59\t     this port.\n60\t2. **`rewriteDocument` becomes true copy-on-write: the analyst's position.** The guide\n61\t   already promises copy-on-write in the `MarkdownRewriteHandler` type row\n62\t   (`guides/markdown.md:41`), the `rewriteDocument` helper row (`:99`), and the `map`\n63\t   method row (`:177`), while the engine rebuilds every parent unconditionally\n64\t   (`src/core/helpers.ts:2377-2384`, `:2475-2569`) — a standing prose-versus-code drift\n65\t   the campaign's honesty laws close one way or the other. The engine changes to match\n66\t   the promise and the html sibling: an unchanged subtree keeps its identity, a changed\n67\t   one rebuilds, and the derivation map records the rebuilt edges. The pinned rows that\n68\t   assert unconditional copying (`tests/src/core/helpers.test.ts:1616-1625`,\n69\t   `tests/src/core/Markdown.test.ts:113-118`) are re-ruled deliberately, red-first, in\n70\t   the unit that owns the engine — a named contract change, not a side effect. The\n71\t   planner's declined-deliberately caution is honored by the naming: the unit's brief\n72\t   states the allocation contract it changes and why.\n73\t3. **`parseInline` stays the document-independent string API** (both lanes converged):\n74\t   the provenance walk drives the same `scanInline` engine with offset-bearing\n75\t   fragments, and no scanner is duplicated.\n76\t\n77\t## Units and routing\n78\t\n79\tWriters serialize in `/home/user/markdown`; `src/core/helpers.ts` and its test file are\n80\tcontended and granted in order.\n81\t\n82\t| Unit | Role and engine | Subject |\n83\t|---|---|---|\n84\t| H2-U1 | `implementer` — Claude Opus 5, native | `src/core/types.ts`: `MarkdownSpan`, `MarkdownSegment`, `MarkdownSource`, `MarkdownParseResult`, `MarkdownDerivation`, `span` on `MarkdownInterface`, full TSDoc |\n85\t| H2-U2 | `sol` bridge — GPT-5.6 Sol, bench | The coordinate engine: `splitLines` walk, `sliceSource`, `joinSources`, `projectSpan`, `stripQuote` and `extractHeading` carriers, guide signature rows |\n86\t| H2-U3 | `sol` bridge — GPT-5.6 Sol, bench | Spans through both walks: `parseBlocks` on sources, the per-construct threading table, `parseProvenance`, `coalesceText` law, red-first per node type |\n87\t| H2-U4 | `sol` bridge — GPT-5.6 Sol, bench | The rewrite engine: identity-preserving reuse, derivation recording, the join and normalization derivation rows, the re-ruled pinned rows |\n88\t| H2-U5 | `implementer` — Claude Opus 5, native | The handle: `#spans`, `span`, `#derive` with the read-source-first ordering, `map` resolution, factories, handle tests |\n89\t| H2-U6 | `implementer` — Claude Opus 5, native | `guides/markdown.md` and parity: the provenance passage, the `span` method row, the changed signatures, the projection exclusion |\n90\t| H2-U7 | `verifier` — Sonnet, native | The authoritative gates |\n91\t\n92\tThe audit round: `analyst` (Sol) over U1's contract fit and U5's derivation ordering (the\n93\tunits Opus writes), `reviewer` (Opus) over U2, U3, and U4 (the units Sol writes), with\n94\tthe second lane per the loop's triggers. The planner's risk list binds the unit briefs:\n95\tthe trailing-space hard-break run, the segment lookup cost (an Orchestrator timing\n96\tobservation after U3), the `splitLines` behavioral pins red-first before the walk lands,\n97\tthe guide rows scoped to the unit changing each signature, the `#derive` ordering case,\n98\tand the `MarkdownSource` naming clarified in the guide's pipeline passage.\n99\t\n100\t## Dropped findings, on the record\n101\t\n102\t- The analyst's `U+0000 → U+FFFD` normalization instruction — dropped as a design input\n103\t  because the probe falsified its premise; preserved as the register entry above because\n104\t  the conformance question is real.\n105\t- The planner's declined-deliberately position on `rewriteDocument` — overruled by the\n106\t  guide's own standing promise; nothing else of its Axis 3 ruling is dropped.\n107\t"}]},"uuid":"87c4cf17-6bdf-4651-bce6-100d02d2874a","timestamp":"2026-08-26T08:05:21.365Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b29f4393-6d57-42b3-bfb2-24e13d39d962","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote_mobile","cwd":"/home/user/markdown","sessionId":"e44afe43-d783-57c4-9b94-e1b722b0b4a2","version":"2.1.246","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"zany-launching-leaf"}

{"parentUuid":"0f8e98ab-2eb8-4a00-a3e8-6993e3fb20f4","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"087400fb-8173-483a-91fe-ccaa67ffaa7b","agentId":"ae24d09dc33db3b10","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01F6T8cLYfDuAK76niMtiBne","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Objective-lane ruling\n2\t\n3\t## Span coordinate system and parse thread\n4\t\n5\t**Position:** Use half-open UTF-16 offsets into the original constructor string. Normalize the parser’s working source while retaining a boundary map back to the original string. Add `parseProvenance(markdown): MarkdownParseResult`; keep `parseDocument` as its document-only projection.\n6\t\n7\tRecommended contract:\n8\t\n9\t```ts\n10\tinterface MarkdownSpan {\n11\t\treadonly start: number\n12\t\treadonly end: number\n13\t}\n14\t\n15\ttype MarkdownParseResult = readonly [\n16\t\tdocument: MarkdownDocument,\n17\t\tspans: ReadonlyMap<MarkdownNode, MarkdownSpan>,\n18\t]\n19\t```\n20\t\n21\t`parseProvenance` must:\n22\t\n23\t- Convert CRLF and lone CR to `\\n`.\n24\t- Replace `U+0000` with `U+FFFD`.\n25\t- Record every normalized boundary’s original UTF-16 offset.\n26\t- Parse through the existing block and inline walks.\n27\t- Project each consumed normalized range through the boundary map before storing it.\n28\t\n29\tThe current `splitLines` normalizes line endings and returns strings without terminators or offsets (`src/core/helpers.ts:70-88`). `parseDocument` passes those strings into `parseBlocks` (`src/core/parsers.ts:125-127`). Provenance therefore requires source fragments that retain text plus boundary offsets. Do not infer offsets later from trimmed values.\n30\t\n31\tMarker stripping and rebuilding must operate on mapped fragments:\n32\t\n33\t- A heading owns the complete physical line. Its inline fragment starts after the opening marker and ends before stripped closing markers and edge whitespace (`src/core/helpers.ts:127-134`; `src/core/parsers.ts:66-72`).\n34\t- A blockquote owns the marked source lines. Nested content uses fragments sliced past each quote marker rather than the strings returned by `stripQuote` (`src/core/helpers.ts:199-213`; `src/core/parsers.ts:76-83`).\n35\t- A list and each list item own their marker and continuation source. Nested blocks receive mapped slices past the applicable indentation (`src/core/helpers.ts:703-787`).\n36\t- A table owns its header, delimiter, and body lines. Inline cell fragments map through pipe splitting, escape handling, and trimming (`src/core/helpers.ts:215-248`, `:662-686`).\n37\t- A paragraph owns the physical run from the first retained line through the last retained line. Inline children map through the per-line trimmed fragments and the original intervening line terminators; the current trim-and-join rebuild loses that relation (`src/core/parsers.ts:97-113`).\n38\t- A fenced code block owns its opening fence through its closing fence, or through input end when unclosed. Its `code` value may exclude fences while its span includes them (`src/core/parsers.ts:45-59`).\n39\t- The document span is `[0, markdown.length]`, including an input trailing terminator that `splitLines` currently removes (`src/core/helpers.ts:84-87`).\n40\t\n41\t`parseInline` may remain the document-independent string API. The provenance parser must use the same scanner with an offset-bearing fragment, not duplicate `scanInline` (`src/core/helpers.ts:568-646`). Existing plain parsing should project the AST from that shared provenance-capable engine, as HTML’s `parseDocument` projects `parseProvenance` (`/home/user/html/src/core/parsers.ts:36-48`).\n42\t\n43\t**Risk:** Reconstructing offsets from node values is unsound because escapes, code-span padding, heading trimming, table escapes, paragraph trimming, and marker removal change spelling or length (`src/core/helpers.ts:320-331`, `:376-403`, `:467-469`, `:581-595`).\n44\t\n45\t## Carrier\n46\t\n47\t**Position:** Keep provenance on each `Markdown` handle in a private identity map. Add the single-word method `span(node): MarkdownSpan | undefined`. Nodes gain no field.\n48\t\n49\tFor string construction, store the document and a copied operation-owned span map from `parseProvenance`. For adopted `MarkdownDocument` input, retain the existing document identity and start with an empty map. Current construction distinguishes parsing from adoption at `src/core/Markdown.ts:41-49`; adoption by reference is tested at `tests/src/core/Markdown.test.ts:31-35`.\n50\t\n51\t`span` must return a fresh `{ start, end }` value, matching the HTML precedent (`/home/user/html/src/core/HTML.ts:89-98`). The public types belong in `src/core/types.ts`; the method belongs on `MarkdownInterface`, whose current surface has no provenance accessor (`src/core/types.ts:353-385`).\n52\t\n53\tThe first real consumer is a caller using `Markdown.find`, `filter`, or `walk` and then locating the returned node in the constructor source. `parseProvenance` serves `Markdown` construction and callers that need a document plus operation-owned spans without a handle.\n54\t\n55\t**Risk:** A process-global or factory-owned map would mix independent parses of structurally equal or shared nodes. Provenance must remain handle-relative.\n56\t\n57\t## Derivation under rebuilt parents\n58\t\n59\t**Position:** Change the shared rewrite engine to preserve identity for an unchanged subtree and return operation-owned derivations for rebuilt nodes. `Markdown.map` must resolve those derivations against the source handle’s span map when it creates the new handle.\n60\t\n61\tUse the HTML vocabulary and shape deliberately:\n62\t\n63\t```ts\n64\ttype MarkdownDerivation<T> = readonly [\n65\t\tvalue: T,\n66\t\tderivations: ReadonlyMap<MarkdownNode, MarkdownNode | undefined>,\n67\t]\n68\t```\n69\t\n70\tA rebuilt node derived from one input node carries that input node’s span. A returned identity already present in the source handle resolves directly. An output identity associated with separate source nodes maps to `undefined`.\n71\t\n72\tThis requires changing `rewriteDocument`. It currently rebuilds every parent and root even when all children and the callback result are unchanged (`src/core/helpers.ts:2377-2384`, `:2457-2473`, `:2475-2569`). Its guide calls this copy-on-write (`guides/markdown.md:177`), while the implementation is unconditional copying. Identity preservation makes the implementation match that term and the HTML precedent (`/home/user/html/src/core/helpers.ts:1294-1311`, `:1350-1395`).\n73\t\n74\t`Markdown.map` must still return a new handle, as currently promised and tested (`src/core/Markdown.ts:92-95`; `tests/src/core/Markdown.test.ts:113-118`). An identity rewrite may reuse the document tree while returning a distinct handle.\n75\t\n76\tAt the depth cap, retain the current unchanged reference and its span. The existing engine already passes capped subtrees through without invoking the callback (`src/core/helpers.ts:2418-2422`).\n77\t\n78\tA slot-mismatch fallback keeps the accepted source child by identity and therefore lawfully keeps its span. A rebuilt parent remains derived from its original parent. The current fallbacks are at `src/core/helpers.ts:2464-2468`, `:2479-2483`, `:2491-2495`, `:2517-2521`, `:2529-2533`, and `:2544-2563`.\n79\t\n80\t**Risk:** Changing `rewriteDocument` from a bare document return to a derivation return affects its existing public consumers and tests. Update every consumer in the same unit; do not add a compatibility engine.\n81\t\n82\t## No-single-source surfaces\n83\t\n84\t**Position:** Promise provenance only for parsed nodes, unchanged identities, and one-source rebuilds. A join or synthetic node has no span.\n85\t\n86\tApply that law as follows:\n87\t\n88\t- `coalesceText`: a newly joined text node drops provenance, even when its inputs happen to be adjacent. Unjoined identities keep their entries. It currently creates a new text node for a join (`src/core/helpers.ts:347-357`).\n89\t- `trimInlines`: an unchanged node may retain provenance. A new text node made by trimming may retain the source text node’s span because it is a one-source rebuild. A dropped node has no output entry (`src/core/helpers.ts:1511-1526`).\n90\t- `normalizeInlines`: pass-through identities retain provenance. Text rebuilt from one text node or one break may inherit that node’s span. Coalesced text from separate nodes drops it. Removed breaks have no output (`src/core/helpers.ts:1553-1586`).\n91\t- `mergeProjections`: paragraphs synthesized from pending siblings have no single source and therefore no span. Child identities passed through may retain provenance only when the calling operation supplied provenance for them (`src/core/helpers.ts:1615-1661`).\n92\t- Slot-mismatch reuse: retain provenance for the reused original child, as an identity rather than a synthesis (`src/core/helpers.ts:2464-2563`).\n93\t- Adopted documents: expose no provenance, including when their nodes happen to be shared with another handle (`src/core/Markdown.ts:43-45`).\n94\t- Standalone helpers invoked on bare nodes do not acquire provenance implicitly. A caller must use an operation returning derivations and resolve them through a handle.\n95\t\n96\tThe guide must state that spans address the original constructor string before CRLF, CR, and null normalization; regions are half-open UTF-16 offsets; parsed nodes cover consumed syntax; one-source rewrites keep the source region; joins, projections, adopted documents, and synthetic nodes return `undefined`.\n97\t\n98\t**Risk:** Assigning a parent’s span to a paragraph synthesized by `mergeProjections` would claim an HTML wrapper or sibling run was markdown source. That violates the no-single-source law.\n99\t\n100\t## Cross-package boundary\n101\t\n102\t**Position:** Exclude `htmlToMarkdown` provenance from this change.\n103\t\n104\tThe installed dependency is `@orkestrel/html@0.0.6` (`node_modules/@orkestrel/html/package.json:1-4`; `package.json:74-77`). Its installed declarations expose no `HTMLSpan`, `parseProvenance`, or `span`. `htmlToMarkdown` accepts only an `HTMLNode` and returns a bare `MarkdownDocument` (`src/core/helpers.ts:2081-2137`). Its fold creates, normalizes, joins, and flattens markdown nodes through `projectHTMLLeaf`, `projectHTMLNode`, and `mergeProjections` (`src/core/helpers.ts:1756-1764`, `:1802-1809`, `:2125-2137`). For stack-safety tests on block guards/traversal.\n274\t */\n275\texport function buildDeepBlockNode(levels: number): unknown {\n276\t\tlet node: unknown = { element: 'paragraph', children: [] }\n277\t\tfor (let depth = 0; depth < levels; depth += 1) {\n278\t\t\tnode = { element: 'blockquote', children: [node] }\n279\t\t}\n280\t\treturn node\n281\t}\n282\t\n283\t// ── Deep markdown source for parser stack-safety tests ────────────────────────\n284\t// Plain string builders producing markdown input nested `levels` deep, for\n285\t// asserting the parser degrades gracefully (never throws) past `MAX_DEPTH`.\n286\t\n287\t/** Markdown source with `levels` leading `>` blockquote markers before `text`. */\n288\texport function buildDeepQuoteInput(levels: number, text = 'leaf'): string {\n289\t\treturn `${'> '.repeat(levels)}${text}`\n290\t}\n291\t\n292\t/** Markdown source for an `levels`-deep nested list, one indent per level. */\n293\texport function buildDeepListInput(levels: number, text = 'leaf'): string {\n294\t\tconst lines: string[] = []\n295\t\tfor (let depth = 0; depth < levels; depth += 1) {\n296\t\t\tlines.push(`${'  '.repeat(depth)}- ${depth === levels - 1 ? text : ''}`)\n297\t\t}\n298\t\treturn lines.join('\\n')\n299\t}\n300\t\n301\t/** Markdown source with `levels` nested `*emphasis*`/`[link](` inline markers around `text`. */\n302\texport function buildDeepEmphasisInput(levels: number, text = 'leaf'): string {\n303\t\tlet source = text\n304\t\tfor (let depth = 0; depth < levels; depth += 1) {\n305\t\t\tsource = depth % 2 === 0 ? `*${source}*` : `[${source}](url)`\n306\t\t}\n307\t\treturn source\n308\t}\n309\t"}]},"uuid":"cad82102-d585-48f8-8823-de22675a29c1","timestamp":"2026-08-26T08:05:34.106Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"971b0511-ab05-495f-b915-3af2b0178d37","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote_mobile","cwd":"/home/user/markdown","sessionId":"e44afe43-d783-57c4-9b94-e1b722b0b4a2","version":"2.1.246","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"zany-launching-leaf"}

The provenance a parse records for a node and\n41\t * {@link MarkdownInterface.span} reads back.\n42\t *\n43\t * @remarks\n44\t * The coordinates address the string the handle was constructed from, never the\n45\t * line text a later phase walks, so `markdown.slice(span.start, span.end)` returns\n46\t * the source the node was parsed from. The region's length is `end - start`; no\n47\t * length member exists to drift from the two offsets.\n48\t */\n49\texport interface MarkdownSpan {\n50\t\t/** The first code unit of the region, inclusive. */\n51\t\treadonly start: number\n52\t\t/** The code unit one past the region's last, exclusive. */\n53\t\treadonly end: number\n54\t}\n55\t\n56\t/**\n57\t * One run of a {@link MarkdownSource} - the mapping from a stretch of derived text\n58\t * back to the region of the ORIGINAL markdown string it was taken from.\n59\t *\n60\t * @remarks\n61\t * `offset` addresses {@link MarkdownSource.text}; `start` and `end` address the\n62\t * original string. The run's original length derives from `end - start` rather than\n63\t * being stored beside them, so no length member exists to drift, and a position `p`\n64\t * inside the run projects back to `start + (p - offset)`. The run's DERIVED extent\n65\t * ends where the next segment's `offset` begins, so a run may cover more of the\n66\t * original than it holds derived: the separator run `joinSources` records over a\n67\t * normalized `\\r\\n` terminator is one derived code unit over a two-unit original\n68\t * region, and the run's end boundary claims that whole region. Every strip, trim, and\n69\t * join the block phase performs is affine at this granularity, which is what lets a\n70\t * derived offset carry an original coordinate.\n71\t */\n72\texport interface MarkdownSegment {\n73\t\t/** The first code unit of the run inside {@link MarkdownSource.text}. */\n74\t\treadonly offset: number\n75\t\t/** The first code unit of the original-string region the run came from, inclusive. */\n76\t\treadonly start: number\n77\t\t/** The code unit one past that region's last, exclusive. */\n78\t\treadonly end: number\n79\t}\n80\t\n81\t/**\n82\t * A piece of derived markdown text paired with the runs mapping it back to the\n83\t * original string - what `splitLines` returns per line, so every phase downstream of\n84\t * it keeps original coordinates instead of reconstructing them from node values.\n85\t *\n86\t * @remarks\n87\t * `text` is the line a parser reads: its terminator, `>` quote marker, or leading\n88\t * indent already removed. `segments` cover `text` in ascending `offset` order, one\n89\t * run per contiguous stretch of the original; a piece assembled from separate\n90\t * stretches carries one segment per stretch.\n91\t */\n92\texport interface MarkdownSource {\n93\t\t/** The derived text a parser reads. */\n94\t\treadonly text: string\n95\t\t/** The runs mapping `text` back to the original string, in ascending `offset` order. */\n96\t\treadonly segments: readonly MarkdownSegment[]\n97\t}\n98\t\n99\t/**\n100\t * A run of plain text - the leaf inline node. `value` is the decoded text with\n101\t * markdown escapes (`\\*`, `\\_`, …) already resolved to their literal characters;\n102\t * html's text encoder escapes `&`, `<`, `>` on the way out; `\"` and `'` stay literal\n103\t * in character data.\n104\t */\n105\texport interface TextNode {\n106\t\treadonly element: 'text'\n107\t\t/** The literal text content (escapes resolved, NOT yet HTML-escaped). */\n108\t\treadonly value: string\n109\t}\n110\t\n111\t/**\n112\t * Emphasized inline content - `*italic*` / `_italic_` (`strong: false`) or\n113\t * `**bold**` / `__bold__` (`strong: true`). `children` are the nested inline nodes,\n114\t * so emphasis composes (a `**bold _and italic_**` is a strong node wrapping a text\n115\t * node and an emphasis node).\n116\t */\n117\texport interface EmphasisNode {\n118\t\treadonly element: 'emphasis'\n119\t\t/** `true` for strong (`**` / `__`, → `<strong>`); {"parentUuid":"40edce96-8be0-4df3-a2d2-d985a984daa7","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"087400fb-8173-483a-91fe-ccaa67ffaa7b","agentId":"ae24d09dc33db3b10","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Kqbx2RNfdSEvUx6mqmA3a5","type":"tool_result","content":"55\t} from '@orkestrel/html'\n56\t\n57\t//  Markdown parsing + rendering leaves (pure and total)\n58\t//\n59\t// The pure leaf primitives {@link parseDocument} composes: the line / block\n60\t// scanners (headings, fences, list items, table rows, quotes, thematic breaks), the\n61\t// `collect*` construct scanners (GFM tables and lists), the inline `scan*` engine\n62\t// (emphasis / links / code with backslash escapes), and the HTML AST projection the\n63\t// renderer composes with @orkestrel/html. Every function is PURE, TOTAL, and\n64\t// referentially transparent - malformed input degrades to text, never throws (AGENTS\n65\t// §14) - so each is unit-tested in isolation. '').trim()))\n464\t\t\tcells.pop()\n465\t\treturn cells\n466\t}\n467\t\n468\t/**\n469\t * Splits an offset-bearing GFM table row into offset-bearing cells, retaining the\n470\t * complete source spelling of an escaped pipe while exposing its literal value.\n471\t *\n472\t * @param row - The offset-bearing table row\n473\t * @returns The row's cells with their original-string coordinates\n474\t *\n475\t * @example\n476\t * ```ts\n477\t * splitTableSources(splitLines('| a\\\\|b |')[0]).map((cell) => cell.text) // [' a|b ']\n478\t * ```\n479\t */\n480\texport function splitTableSources(row: MarkdownSource): readonly MarkdownSource[] {\n481\t\tconst source = trimSource(row)\n482\t\tconst cells: MarkdownSource[] = []\n483\t\tlet pieces: MarkdownSource[] = []\n484\t\tlet start = 0\n485\t\tfor (let index = 0; index < source.text.length; index += 1) {\n486\t\t\tconst character = source.text[index]\n487\t\t\tif (character === '\\\\' && source.text[index + 1] === '|') {\n488\t\t\t\tpieces.push(sliceSource(source, start, index))\n489\t\t\t\tconst span = projectSpan(source, index, index + 2)\n490\t\t\t\tpieces.push({\n491\t\t\t\t\ttext: '|',\n492\t\t\t\t\tsegments: span === undefined ? Rather than guess, every node reports each view an ancestor could want, and the ancestor that knows the context takes the one it needs:\n455:| `text`    | raw subtree text — whitespace uncollapsed, escapes unresolved       | a `code` span, a `pre > code` body               |\n470:| `code`                        | a code span, from the raw subtree text with newline runs collapsed to one space                                            |\n493:- **Block content in a table cell flattens.** Markdown has no way to put a paragraph inside a cell, so a cell's blocks become one text node of their words, joined and whitespace-collapsed: a `<td>` holding `<p>a</p><p>b</p>` becomes the cell `a b`.\n517:// House rule: <kbd>Esc</kbd> reads as a code span. Every other element keeps the default.\n525:\t\t\tinlines: [{ element: 'codeSpan', value: merged.text }],\n557:- **Leaf shapes + compiled contracts, in lockstep.** `shapers.ts` declares `ContractShape` values (`textShape`, `codeSpanShape`, `lineBreakShape`, `codeBlockShape`, `thematicBreakShape`, `tableAlignShape`, `listItemMatchShape`) for the AST's non-recursive node types. `factories.ts` compiles five of them through `createContract` into `ContractInterface<T>` bundles — `schema` / `is` / `parse` / `generate` derived from one declaration, so they can never drift from each other.\n646:\tdocument: (_, children) => children.join('\\n'),\n647:\theading: (node, children) => `<h${node.level}>${children.join('')}</h${node.level}>`,\n648:\tparagraph: (_, children) => `<p>${children.join('')}</p>`,\n650:\tblockquote: (_, children) => `<blockquote>${children.join('\\n')}</blockquote>`,\n653:\t\tnode.ordered ? `<ol>${children.join('')}</ol>` : `<ul>${children.join('')}</ul>`,\n654:\tlistItem: (_, children) => `<li>${children.join('')}</li>`,\n655:\ttable: (_, children) => `<table>${children.join('')}</table>`,\n658:\t\tnode.strong ? `<strong>${children.join('')}</strong>` : `<em>${children.join('')}</em>`,\n659:\tcodeSpan: (node) => `<code>${node.value}</code>`,\n661:\tlink: (node, children) => `<a href=\"${node.href}\">${children.join('')}</a>`,\n662:\timage: (node, children) => `<img src=\"${node.src}\" alt=\"${children.join('')}\">`,\n776:\tcodeSpan: () => 1,\n795:const fragment = parseInline('a **bold** span') // readonly InlineNode[], no block structure\n823:anything (§ [Source provenance](#source-provenance))."}]},"uuid":"71ef6721-a41d-4313-8ebe-aaca2b76db97","timestamp":"2026-08-26T08:06:23.355Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"567e234b-b65b-4989-8b04-9829a65ff67b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote_mobile","cwd":"/home/user/markdown","sessionId":"e44afe43-d783-57c4-9b94-e1b722b0b4a2","version":"2.1.246","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"zany-launching-leaf"}

{"parentUuid":"424259eb-20b6-45bf-885d-4a0e80cb68e2","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"087400fb-8173-483a-91fe-ccaa67ffaa7b","agentId":"ae24d09dc33db3b10","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KcY8kgwb5NrmH9195uxCWF","type":"tool_result","content":"249\t## Source provenance\n250\t\n251\tA parse records the region of the source each node came from, and the `Markdown` instance that ran\n252\tthe parse reads those regions back through `span`:\n253\t\n254\t```ts\n255\timport { Markdown, isHeadingNode } from '@orkestrel/markdown'\n256\t\n257\tconst source = '# Title\\n\\nA **bold** word.'\n258\tconst markdown = new Markdown(source)\n259\t\n260\tconst heading = markdown.find(isHeadingNode)\n261\tconst region = heading === undefined ? undefined : markdown.span(heading)\n262\tregion // { start: 0, end: 7 }\n263\tif (region !== undefined) source.slice(region.start, region.end) // '# Title'\n264\t```\n265\t\n266\tThese rules fix what a region means, and every one of them is about the string the instance was\n267\tconstructed from:\n268\t\n269\t- **A region addresses the ORIGINAL constructor string**, never the line text a later phase walks.\n270\t  `source.slice(region.start, region.end)` returns the source the node was parsed from.\n271\t- **A region is half-open and counted in UTF-16 code units** — `start` inclusive, `end` exclusive.\n272\t  Its length derives from `end - start`; no length member exists to drift from the two offsets.\n273\t- **A parsed node covers the syntax it consumed**, markers included. The heading in the preceding\n274\t  fence starts at `0`, not at `2` where its text starts, and an emphasis node covers its `**`\n275\t  delimiters.\n276\t- **A one-source rewrite keeps its source's region through `map`.** A node the handler replaced\n277\t  from one input node reports that input's region; a rebuilt ancestor reports its original's.\n278\t- **A node with no single source region reports `undefined`** — a foreign node, every node of an\n279\t  adopted document, and a rewrite output assembled from separate source nodes.\n280\t\n281\t**Line endings normalize at line granularity, and nothing else does.** `splitLines` treats `\\r\\n`\n282\tand a lone `\\r` as terminators and drops them with the line boundary, so a region can span a\n283\tterminator the derived text no longer holds — that is what the separator segment `joinSources`\n284\trecords is for. Within a line the parser rewrites no character at all: an escape stays decoded in\n285\tthe node's `value` and undecoded in the region, so a region always slices the constructor string\n286\tverbatim. Read provenance off the source; never reconstruct it from a node value.\n287\t\n288\t```ts\n289\timport { Markdown, isTextNode } from '@orkestrel/markdown'\n290\t\n291\tconst source = 'a \\\\* b *c*'\n292\tconst markdown = new Markdown(source)\n293\t\n294\tconst [text] = markdown.filter(isTextNode)\n295\ttext?.value // 'a * b ' — the escape decoded\n296\tconst region = text === undefined ? undefined : markdown.span(text)\n297\tregion // { start: 0, end: 7 } — the region slices the spelling `a \\* b `, not the value\n298\t```\n299\t\n300\t**`parseProvenance` is the handle-free entry point.** It returns the document and the\n301\toperation-owned span map together, so a caller that wants coordinates without a `Markdown` instance\n302\tgets both from one parse. `parseDocument` is its document projection, and constructing a `Markdown`\n303\tfrom a string runs it once and copies the map into the instance.\n304\t\n305\t```ts\n306\timport { parseProvenance } from '@orkestrel/markdown'\n307\t\n308\tconst [document, spans] = parseProvenance('# Title\\n\\nA **bold** word.')\n309\tspans.get(document) // { start: 0, end: 25 } — the whole input\n310\t```\n311\t\n312\tThe map is keyed by node identity, so it addresses that document's nodes and no other. Two\n313\tinstances over the same text hold independent maps, and a node from one reports `undefined` in the\n314\tother.\n315\t\n316\t**A rewrite carries regions forward through its derivations.** `rewriteDocument` returns a\n317\t`MarkdownDerivation`, and `map` resolves each output node against the source instance's map: an\n318\toutput that kept its identity keeps the region it had, an output derived from one input node takes\n319\tthat input's region, and an output derived from separate inputs takes none.\n320\t\n321\t```ts\n322\timport { Markdown, isTextNode } from '@orkestrel/markdown'\n323\t\n324\tconst markdown = new Markdown('# Hi\\n\\nText.')\n325\tconst lowered = markdown.map((node) =>\n326\t\tnode.element === 'text' ? { element: 'text', value: node.value.toLowerCase() } : node,\n327\t)\n328\t\n329\tconst [first] = lowered.filter(isTextNode)\n330\tfirst?.value // 'hi'\n331\tconst region = first === undefined ? undefined : lowered.span(first)\n332\tregion // { start: 2, end: 4 } — where `Hi` sits in the ORIGINAL source\n333\t```\n334\t\n335\t**Where provenance stops, and why.** An adopted document, the inbound projection, and a rewrite\n336\toutput assembled from separate sources each report `undefined` rather than an approximate region,\n337\tbecause an approximate region is worse than none — it claims the author wrote something they did\n338\tnot:\n339\t\n340\t- **An adopted document.** `new Markdown(document)` parsed no string, so no coordinates exist to\n341\t  report. Adoption keeps the tree by reference and starts with an empty map, including where those\n342\t  nodes are shared with an instance that does have regions.\n343\t- **The inbound projection.** `htmlToMarkdown` folds an HTML node, which carries no markdown\n344\t  coordinates, so nothing it emits has a region — a paragraph `mergeProjections` synthesizes from a\n345\t  run of pending siblings least of all. That paragraph has no single source even in principle: it\n346\t  was assembled from an HTML wrapper's children, not written as a paragraph anywhere.\n347\t- **A rewrite output assembled from separate sources.** One node returned for several input nodes\n348\t  is covered by no single region of the original, so `map` resolves it to `undefined`.\n349\t\n350\t```ts\n351\timport { Markdown, htmlToMarkdown, isTextNode } from '@orkestrel/markdown'\n352\timport { parseDocument as parseHTML } from '@orkestrel/html'\n353\t\n354\tconst imported = new Markdown(htmlToMarkdown(parseHTML('<div>text<p>para</p></div>')))\n355\timported.span(imported.document) // undefined — adopted, and projected from HTML\n356\t\n357\tconst markdown = new Markdown('a *b* c')\n358\tconst joined = { element: 'text', value: 'joined' } as const\n359\tconst merged = markdown.map((node) => (node.element === 'text' ? joined : node))\n360\tconst [text] = merged.filter(isTextNode)\n361\ttext === undefined ? undefined : merged.span(text) // undefined — one output, separate sources\n362\t```\n363\t\n364\t`span` builds its return value fresh on every call, so a caller who mutates the object it hands\n365\tback changes nothing the next call reports.\n366\t\n367\t### Coordinates inside a line\n368\t\n369\tThe block phase carries `MarkdownSource` values rather than strings (§ [The parse\n370\tpipeline](#the-parse-pipeline)), and the inline engine has an offset-bearing entry point of its own.\n371\t`scanInlineSource` runs the same scan `scanInline` does and records each node it emits into a\n372\trecorder the caller owns:\n373\t\n374\t```ts\n375\timport { scanInlineSource, splitLines } from '@orkestrel/markdown'\n376\timport type { MarkdownNode, MarkdownSpan } from '@orkestrel/markdown'\n377\t\n378\tconst [line] = splitLines('> a *b*')\n379\tconst spans = new Map<MarkdownNode, MarkdownSpan>()\n380\tconst nodes = line === undefined ? [] : scanInlineSource(line, 2, line.text.length, spans, 0)\n381\t\n382\tconst [text, emphasis] = nodes\n383\ttext === undefined ? undefined : spans.get(text) // { start: 2, end: 4 } — `a `\n384\temphasis === undefined ? undefined : spans.get(emphasis) // { start: 4, end: 7 } — `*b*`\n385\t```\n386\t\n387\tEvery coordinate leaf is exported for the same reason the projection leaves are: a caller writing\n388\ttheir own block or inline phase over the same lines needs `sliceSource`, `trimSource`,\n389\t`joinSources`, `normalizeParagraphLine`, `splitTableSources`, and `projectSpan` to keep coordinates\n390\tthe way the shipped phases do.\n391\t\n392\t## Sanitization policy\n393\t"}]},"uuid":"4686435e-36ac-4462-a382-859d36783551","timestamp":"2026-08-26T08:06:25.477Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"424259eb-20b6-45bf-885d-4a0e80cb68e2","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote_mobile","cwd":"/home/user/markdown","sessionId":"e44afe43-d783-57c4-9b94-e1b722b0b4a2","version":"2.1.246","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"zany-launching-leaf"}

The document span is `[0, markdown.length]`,\n47-  the removed trailing terminator included. `parseInline` keeps its document-independent\n48-  string signature (design ruling: the provenance walk drives the same `scanInline`\n49-  engine with offset-bearing fragments; no scanner is duplicated).\n50:- The `coalesceText` law (`src/core/helpers.ts:519`): where adjacent text fragments\n51:  coalesce, the recorded span covers the whole coalesced run against the original.\n52-- The planner risk bound to this unit: the trailing-space hard-break run — the trailing\n53-  spaces a hard break consumes belong to the break's consumed syntax; pin its exact\n54-  span red-first.\n55-- The map is keyed by node identity, so the walk must record against the exact node\n--\n65-\n66-Per node type — heading, paragraph, blockquote, list and item, table, fence, thematic\n67-break, and the inline families through the fragment walk — add the span assertion\n68-red-first against the tree before your threading lands, slicing the ORIGINAL string at\n69:the recorded offsets as the proof, then green after. The coalesced-run case and the\n70-trailing-space hard-break case are named rows. Pin also: a parse whose node VALUES are\n71-byte-identical to the pre-threading parse for a document exercising every construct —\n72-provenance is additive; values never move.\n73-\n--\n144-1. `git diff --check` exits 0 and the diff touches only owned files.\n145-2.
